## User prompt

```text
Goal: replace SQLAlchemy with Tortoise ORM across the Mealie backend.

Fixed decisions:
- Use Tortoise ORM, which is async. When the rewrite is finished, nothing under mealie/
  should import SQLAlchemy.
- Keep support for both SQLite and PostgreSQL.
- Replace Alembic with Aerich. Upgrading existing databases from the old Alembic history
  is out of scope.
- Keep the HTTP API and the Pydantic schemas in mealie/schema unchanged, so the frontend
  does not need to change.
- Database access becomes async. Make routes, services and scheduler tasks async where
  they need to be.
- You may change how the tests set up and access the database (tests/conftest.py,
  tests/fixtures/, and database setup code inside test files). You must not delete tests,
  skip them, mark them xfail, remove or weaken assertions, or change expected values.
- Run the tests with: uv run pytest -n 4 --dist loadfile
  (tests in the same file depend on each other, so they must run grouped by file).
- Do not push, and do not change git remotes or git configuration.

Read AGENTS.md first, especially the "Tortoise ORM rewrite" section. Then do step C7
from that section and nothing else. Follow the conventions and decisions recorded there.
When you finish, run the tests, then update the "Tortoise ORM rewrite" section: record any
new convention or decision, and update the progress table.
```

so turning it off down the line doesn't revert back to the original journal mode.\n\n    Write-Ahead-Log enables sqlite to be used concurrently by multiple readers and writers (writes still happen\n dbapi_connection.cursor()\n    cursor.execute(\"PRAGMA synchronous=OFF\")\n    if not settings.SQLITE_MIGRATE_JOURNAL_WAL:\n        cursor.execute(\"PRAGMA journal_mode=MEMORY\")\n {

  "aggregated_output": "from slugify import slugify\nfrom sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom mealie.core import root_logger\nfrom mealie.db.models._legacy_groups import Group\n\nlogger = root_logger.get_logger(\"init_db\")\n\n\ndef _do_fix(session: Session, group: Group, counter: int):\n    if counter:\n        new_name = f\"{group.id} ({counter})\"\n    else:\n        new_name = str(group.id)\n\n    group.name = new_name\n    group.slug = slugify(group.name)\n    session.commit()\n\n\ndef fix_group_with_no_name(session: Session):\n    groups = session.query(Group).filter(Group.name == \"\").all()\n    if not groups:\n        logger.debug(\"No group found with an empty name; skipping fix\")\n        return\n\n    logger.info(\n        f\"{len(groups)} {'group' if len(groups) == 1 else 'groups'} found with a missing name; applying default name\"\n    )\n\n    offset = 0\n    for i, ## agent_message at 2026-10-11T10:58:22.405958+00:00

Expression(f\"({self.sql} OR {other.sql})\", self.parameters + other.parameters)\n\n    def __invert__(self) -> Expression:\n        return Expression(f\"NOT ({self.sql})\", self.parameters)\n\n \"FALSE\")\n    return Expression(\n        \"(\" + f\" {operator} \".join(item.sql for item in expressions) + \")\",\n        [value for item in expressions for value in item.parameters],\n    )\n\n\ndef column(model: type[Model], name: str) -> str:\n    return f'\"{model._meta.db_table}\".\"{model._meta.fields_db_projection.get(name, name)}\"'\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Relation:\n    source: ## agent_message at 2026-10-11T11:04:04.999057+00:00

Review caught two edge cases: backup restore must leave substitution references intact to satisfy their CHECK constraints, and nested filters need aliases when they reach the same table twice. Both are fixed with regression coverage added. The test command confirms the current blocker is `ModuleNotFoundError: aerich`; the schema audit still shows unchanged Pydantic fields, validators, and serializers.

 schema_generator = MealieSqliteSchemaGenerator\n\n    def _in_transaction(self) -> TransactionContext:\n        return NestedTransactionContext(MealieSqliteTransactionWrapper(self))\n\n    @translate_exceptions\n    async def execute_script(self, query: str) -> None:\n        # sqlite3.executescript implicitly commits an existing transaction.\n        # Execute complete statements individually so Aerich's schema changes\n        # and its migration ledger commit together, including on failure.\n        async with self.acquire_connection() as connection:\n            statement = \"\"\n            for character in query:\n                statement += character\n                if character == \";\" and sqlite3.complete_statement(statement):\n                    await connection.execute(statement)\n                    statement = \"\"\n            if statement.strip():\n                await connection.execute(statement)\n\n\nclass MealieSqliteClient(SqliteClient):\n    schema_generator = MealieSqliteSchemaGenerator\n\n    def __init__(self, file_path: str, **kwargs: Any) -> None:\n        super().__init__(file_path, **kwargs)\n        # Tortoise defaults to setting WAL on every connection. When Mealie's\n        # setting is off, leave the existing persistent journal mode untouched.\n        if \"journal_mode\" not in kwargs:\n            self.pragmas.pop(\"journal_mode\", None)\n\n    def _in_transaction(self) -> TransactionContext:\n        return SqliteTransactionContext(MealieSqliteTransactionWrapper(self), self._lock)\n\n\nclient_class = MealieSqliteClient\n",

## agent_message at 2026-10-11T11:05:45.916884+00:00

Type checking and backend lint now pass. The independent schema review also found that Tortoise makes join-table columns non-null by default; I’m preserving their existing nullability. PostgreSQL fuzzy search now sets its threshold within the transaction that runs the query, so connection pooling cannot apply it to the wrong connection.
